{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HBBqXRGMBYr1boSAidcGFf","type":"tool_result","content":"import type {AddressInfo} from 'node:net';\nimport {WebSocket} from 'ws';\nimport type {Express} from 'express';\nimport {createServer} from './createServer.js';\nimport type {HalServer, HalServerOptions} from './createServer.js';\nimport {HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL} from './ws/subprotocol.js';\nimport type {ChatOrchestrator} from '../orchestration/chatOrchestrator.js';\nimport type {MessageChunk} from '../types/ai.js';\nimport type {ChatSession} from '../types/session.js';\nimport type {SessionStore} from '../types/sessionStore.js';\n\n// Shared by the suites that need a real upgrade over a real socket, which no double can stand in for.\n\nconst servers: Array<{stop: () => Promise<void>}> = [];\nconst clients: WebSocket[] = [];\n\n/** An orchestrator that answers once and stops, which is what commits the turn's assistant entry. */\nexport const answeringOrchestrator: ChatOrchestrator = {\n  processMessage: async () => 'hi',\n  async *processMessageStream(): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n    yield {type: 'text', text: 'hi'};\n    yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn'};\n  },\n};\n\n/** Starts an engine on an ephemeral port and returns the URL a client should open. */\nexport async function startEngine(\n  sessionStore: SessionStore,\n  onDisconnect?: (sessionId: string) => void\n): Promise<string> {\n  const {url} = await startEngineWith({sessionStore, onDisconnect});\n  return url;\n}\n\n/** Starts an engine with whatever a suite overrides, returning the server itself beside the URL a client should open. */\nexport async function startEngineWith(\n  options: Partial<HalServerOptions> & Pick<HalServerOptions, 'sessionStore'>\n): Promise<{url: string; hal: HalServer}> {\n  const hal = createServer({\n    app: (() => undefined) as unknown as Express,\n    wsAuth: async () => ({id: 'u1'}),\n    orchestrator: answeringOrchestrator,\n    ...options,\n  });\n  await hal.start(0);\n  servers.push(hal);\n  const {port} = hal.server.address() as AddressInfo;\n  return {url: `ws://127.0.0.1:${port}/hal/ws/c1`, hal};\n}\n\n/** Opens a client that the suite's teardown will close, whether its test passed or failed. */\nexport function connectClient(url: string, token = 't'): WebSocket {\n  const client = new WebSocket(url, [HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL, token]);\n  clients.push(client);\n  return client;\n}\n\n/** A promise a test settles by hand, to hold a stream or a hook at a point of its choosing. */\nexport function deferred(): {until: Promise<void>; release: () => void} {\n  let release = (): void => undefined;\n  const until = new Promise<void>(resolve => {\n    release = resolve;\n  });\n  return {until, release};\n}\n\n/** Resolves with every frame type seen up to and including the first `until` frame. */\nexport function framesUntil(client: WebSocket, until: string): Promise<string[]> {\n  const seen: string[] = [];\n  return new Promise<string[]>(resolve => {\n    client.on('message', raw => {\n      const {type} = JSON.parse(String(raw)) as {type: string};\n      seen.push(type);\n      if (type === until) resolve(seen);\n    });\n  });\n}\n\n/** Opens a client that asks one question the moment the socket opens, resolving on the first `until` frame. */\nexport function askOnOpen(\n  url: string,\n  {until = 'stream_end', token}: {until?: string; token?: string} = {}\n): {client: WebSocket; finished: Promise<string[]>} {\n  const client = connectClient(url, token);\n  const finished = framesUntil(client, until);\n  client.on('open', () => client.send(JSON.stringify({type: 'user_message', content: 'hello'})));\n  return {client, finished};\n}\n\n/** The session id the client's `connected` frame announces, read after the fact; attach it before any frame arrives. */\nexport function announcedId(client: WebSocket): () => string {\n  let sessionId = '';\n  client.on('message', raw => {\n    const frame = JSON.parse(String(raw)) as {type: string; sessionId?: string};\n    if (frame.type === 'connected') sessionId = frame.sessionId ?? '';\n  });\n  return () => sessionId;\n}\n\n/** Tears every socket and server down, so a red test reports instead of hanging the run. */\nexport async function stopEngines(): Promise<void> {\n  for (const client of clients.splice(0)) client.terminate();\n  for (const hal of servers.splice(0)) await hal.stop();\n}\n\n/** The one session a store holds, for asserting what survived a close. */\nexport async function onlySession(store: SessionStore, sessionId: string): Promise<ChatSession | undefined> {\n  return (await store.get(sessionId)) as ChatSession | undefined;\n}\n---\nimport {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01EywFzW4Rz39CLCydLYLuFe","type":"tool_result","content":"35\n48:The server validates only that the path begins with `{basePath}/ws`. The `sessionId` query parameter is read only when the server enables resume (`transport.resume`), and only if it is 1–128 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`. It is not a credential: the server rejoins that conversation only if it belongs to the connection's authenticated user, and answers any other id — someone else's, one it never issued, or one its store no longer holds — with a fresh session exactly as if no id had been sent. With `transport.resume.latest`, a connect that sends no id rejoins the user's most recently active conversation instead, unless it sends `?new=1`, which starts a new one. The server never adopts a requested id for a new session. See [the session resume spec](../hal-engine-session-resume/spec.md).\n71:- An upgrade whose `Host` header is missing or empty (RFC 6455 §4.1 requires one), or whose request-target is not a URL, such as `//`, is answered with HTTP 400 ([validated by: answers an empty Host, a missing Host and a // target with 400 instead of throwing out of the upgrade listener](../../src/transport/malformedUpgrade.test.ts#L68)).\n75:- A `WsAuthenticator` that returns a user directly, not in a promise, is treated like one that resolves to it, rather than failing inside the listener ([validated by: upgrades a request whose authenticator returns a user without a promise](../../src/transport/malformedUpgrade.test.ts#L118)).\n95:The `sessionId` is a UUID v4 assigned by the server. It uniquely identifies this session; with resume enabled it outlives the connection and a reconnect can name it. The `examplePrompts` array contains example queries derived from the registered tool definitions, displayed in the client as clickable suggestions.\n97:With resume enabled, the frame also carries `resumed`. On `resumed: true` it carries `entryCount`, and the server then replays the stored conversation before any other frame: one `entry_upsert` per entry at its own index, or an `entry_skip` for an entry a tool suppressed, so the client sees the conversation it saw live. When the connection named an id and was not resumed, the frame also carries `resumeFailure`: `expired` when the caller's own session aged out, `unknown` for every other case. With resume disabled none of these fields is present.\n175:| `resumed`        | boolean  | Present only with resume enabled: whether the requested conversation was rejoined |\n176:| `entryCount`     | number   | Present only when `resumed` is `true`: how many stored entries the replay covers, skipped ones included |\n177:| `resumeFailure`  | string   | Present only when the connection named an id and `resumed` is `false`: `expired` when the caller's own session aged out, `unknown` otherwise. `unknown` deliberately covers both an id the server never issued and one that is not the caller's, so the answer reveals nothing about ids that exist |\n401:| `truncated`    | `true`  | Optional. Present only when the run ended before this entry finished, or when a resume replays it still streaming; see § 7.1 and § 11.3 |\n420:| `truncated`   | `true`  | Optional. Present only when the run ended before this entry finished, or when a resume replays it still streaming; see § 7.1 and § 11.3 |\n516:**Commit frames are not emitted in index order.** Above, `entry_commit` for index 1 arrives after `entry_upsert` for index 2. A thinking entry is committed when the next text segment arrives, not when a tool entry appears, so a tool call between thinking and the answer lands in the gap. A client that assumes commits arrive in ascending index order, or that an entry is committed before the next one opens, will mis-render this exchange ([validated by: commits the thinking entry when text resumes, not when a tool entry appears](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L134)).\n634:- On reconnection, the client MUST clear its local entries array and SHOULD send `?sessionId=` with the id from its last `connected` frame. When the server has resume enabled and the conversation is the user's own, it answers `resumed: true` and replays the conversation; otherwise the client is starting a new session.\n635:- An entry still streaming when its conversation is resumed is replayed with `isStreaming: false` and `truncated: true`: the rest of its turn streams to the connection that started it, so this one would wait for a commit that never comes ([validated by: replays an entry whose turn is still running as finished and truncated, leaving the stored entry as it is](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L364)).\n644:- No offered subprotocol value is written to the engine's own log at any level. Every place a credential exists during a connection - request headers, the socket's and the session's `authHeaders`, and the tool context - is listed in [the subprotocol marker spec](../hal-engine-subprotocol-marker/spec.md#where-a-credential-exists).\nimport {WebSocket} from 'ws';\nimport type {SessionEntry} from '../../types/session.js';\nimport type {ChatSession} from '../../types/session.js';\nimport {ErrorCodes} from '../../types/messages.js';\nimport type {OutgoingMessage} from '../../types/messages.js';\nimport {ThinkingTagParser, ParsedSegment} from '../../infrastructure/parsers/thinkingTagParser.js';\nimport {validateMessage} from './validation.js';\nimport type {ValidationResult} from './validation.js';\nimport {PerSessionLock} from '../../shared/perSessionLock.js';\nimport type {ChatOrchestrator} from '../../orchestration/chatOrchestrator.js';\nimport {AIError} from '../../types/ai.js';\nimport type {MessageChunk} from '../../types/ai.js';\nimport {\n  createUserEntry,\n  createAssistantEntry,\n  createThinkingEntry,\n  createToolEntry,\n} from '../../orchestration/entryFactories.js';\nimport {appendEntry, appendDelta, commitEntry, markTruncated} from '../../orchestration/entryMutations.js';\nimport {sendJson, sendUpsert, sendDelta, sendCommit, sendSkip, sendError, sendStreamEnd} from './sender.js';\nimport {log} from '../../shared/logger.js';\n\ninterface StreamState {\n  thinkingIndex: number | null;\n  assistantIndex: number | null;\n  suppressOutput: boolean;\n  sentAssistantIndices: number[];\n}\n\ntype StateIndexKey = 'thinkingIndex' | 'assistantIndex';\ntype EntryFactory = typeof createThinkingEntry | typeof createAssistantEntry;\nexport function createMessageHandler(orchestrator: ChatOrchestrator) {\n  // Keyed by session, not socket: a resumed session can be open on more than one (specs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md).\n  const lock = new PerSessionLock();\n  return function handleMessage(\n    ws: WebSocket,\n    session: ChatSession,\n    rawMessage: unknown,\n    signal?: AbortSignal\n  ): Promise<void> {\n    const validation = validateMessage(rawMessage);\n    // Queued with no await before it, so a session's user_messages, a failed one too, are answered in arrival order.\n    if (isUserMessageFrame(rawMessage)) {\n      return lock.run(session.sessionId, () => answerInTurn(ws, session, orchestrator, validation, signal));\n    }\n    // An async action runs up to its first await at once, so a ping is answered now and a throw stays a rejection.\n    return wsErrorHandler(ws, async () => answerFrame(ws, validation));\n  };\n}\n\n// The whole answer, an error and the stream_end included, goes out inside the lock: an server validates only that the path begins with `{basePath}/ws`. The `sessionId` query parameter is read only when the server enables resume (`transport.resume`), and only if it is 1–128 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`. It is not a credential: the server rejoins that conversation only if it belongs to the connection's authenticated user, and answers any other id — someone else's, one it never issued, or one its store no longer holds — with a fresh session exactly as if no id had been sent. With `transport.resume.latest`, a connect that sends no id rejoins the user's most recently active conversation instead, unless it sends `?new=1`, which starts a new one. The server never adopts a requested id for a new session. See [the session resume spec](../hal-engine-session-resume/spec.md).\n71:- An upgrade whose `Host` header is missing or empty (RFC 6455 §4.1 requires one), or whose request-target is not a URL, such as `//`, is answered with HTTP 400 ([validated by: answers an empty Host, a missing Host and a // target with 400 instead of throwing out of the upgrade listener](../../src/transport/malformedUpgrade.test.ts#L68)).\n75:- A `WsAuthenticator` that returns a user directly, not in a promise, is treated like one that resolves to it, rather than failing inside the listener ([validated by: upgrades a request whose authenticator returns a user without a promise](../../src/transport/malformedUpgrade.test.ts#L118)).\n95:The `sessionId` is a UUID v4 assigned by the server. It uniquely identifies this session; with resume enabled it outlives the connection and a reconnect can name it. The `examplePrompts` array contains example queries derived from the registered tool definitions, displayed in the client as clickable suggestions.\n97:With resume enabled, the frame also carries `resumed`. On `resumed: true` it carries `entryCount`, and the server then replays the stored conversation before any other frame: one `entry_upsert` per entry at its own index, or an `entry_skip` for an entry a tool suppressed, so the client sees the conversation it saw live. When the connection named an id and was not resumed, the frame also carries `resumeFailure`: `expired` when the caller's own session aged out, `unknown` for every other case. With resume disabled none of these fields is present.\n175:| `resumed`        | boolean  | Present only with resume enabled: whether the requested conversation was rejoined |\n176:| `entryCount`     | number   | Present only when `resumed` is `true`: how many stored entries the replay covers, skipped ones included |\n177:| `resumeFailure`  | string   | Present only when the connection named an id and `resumed` is `false`: `expired` when the caller's own session aged out, `unknown` otherwise. `unknown` deliberately covers both an id the server never issued and one that is not the caller's, so the answer reveals nothing about ids that exist |\n401:| `truncated`    | `true`  | Optional. Present only when the run ended before this entry finished, or when a resume replays it still streaming; see § 7.1 and § 11.3 |\n420:| `truncated`   | `true`  | Optional. Present only when the run ended before this entry finished, or when a resume replays it still streaming; see § 7.1 and § 11.3 |\n516:**Commit frames are not emitted in index order.** Above, `entry_commit` for index 1 arrives after `entry_upsert` for index 2. A thinking entry is committed when the next text segment arrives, not when a tool entry appears, so a tool call between thinking and the answer lands in the gap. A client that assumes commits arrive in ascending index order, or that an entry is committed before the next one opens, will mis-render this exchange ([validated by: commits the thinking entry when text resumes, not when a tool entry appears](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L134)).\n634:- On reconnection, the client MUST clear its local entries array and SHOULD send `?sessionId=` with the id from its last `connected` frame. When the server has resume enabled and the conversation is the user's own, it answers `resumed: true` and replays the conversation; otherwise the client is starting a new session.\n635:- An entry still streaming when its conversation is resumed is replayed with `isStreaming: false` and `truncated: true`: the rest of its turn streams to the connection that started it, so this one would wait for a commit that never comes ([validated by: replays an entry whose turn is still running as finished and truncated, leaving the stored entry as it is](../../src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts#L364)).\n644:- No offered subprotocol value is written to the engine's own log at any level. Every place a credential exists during a connection - request headers, the socket's and the session's `authHeaders`, and the tool context - is listed in [the subprotocol marker {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014JRN7GMKSK7ibgsyEtEi3p","type":"tool_result","content":"import type {ChatSession} from '../../types/session.js';\nimport type {\n  SessionStore,\n  SessionCreateOptions,\n  SessionListOptions,\n  SessionLookup,\n  SessionSummary,\n} from '../../types/sessionStore.js';\nimport {cappedLimit, cursorAt, newestFirst, sitsAfter} from './sessionListWindow.js';\n\n// Eight hours: a memory bound, not a retention decision (specs/hal-engine-session-lifetime/spec.md).\nconst DEFAULT_MAX_AGE_MS = 8 * 60 * 60 * 1000;\n\nexport interface InMemorySessionStoreOptions {\n  maxAgeMs?: number;\n}\n\n// The creation time is held beside the session rather than on it, so it never reaches the public ChatSession.\ninterface HeldSession {\n  session: ChatSession;\n  createdAt: number;\n}\n\nexport class InMemorySessionStore implements SessionStore {\n  private readonly sessions = new Map<string, HeldSession>();\n  private readonly maxAgeMs: number;\n\n  constructor(options: InMemorySessionStoreOptions = {}) {\n    this.maxAgeMs = options.maxAgeMs ?? DEFAULT_MAX_AGE_MS;\n  }\n\n  create(sessionId: string, userId: string | number, options?: SessionCreateOptions): ChatSession {\n    this.sweepExpired();\n\n    const session: ChatSession = {\n      sessionId,\n      userId,\n      entries: [],\n      authHeaders: options?.authHeaders,\n      workspaceId: options?.workspaceId,\n    };\n    this.sessions.set(sessionId, {session, createdAt: Date.now()});\n    return session;\n  }\n\n  get(sessionId: string): ChatSession | undefined {\n    const held = this.sessions.get(sessionId);\n    if (held === undefined) return undefined;\n    if (!this.hasExpired(held)) return held.session;\n\n    this.sessions.delete(sessionId);\n    return undefined;\n  }\n\n  /** One shot: a session past `maxAgeMs` answers `expired` once and is evicted, never kept as a tombstone. */\n  lookup(sessionId: string): SessionLookup {\n    const held = this.sessions.get(sessionId);\n    if (held === undefined) return {status: 'missing'};\n    if (!this.hasExpired(held)) return {status: 'active', session: held.session};\n\n    this.sessions.delete(sessionId);\n    return {status: 'expired', userId: held.session.userId};\n  }\n\n  delete(sessionId: string): boolean {\n    return this.sessions.delete(sessionId);\n  }\n\n  // Eviction and erasure coincide for a store that is only a cache; they do not for a durable one.\n  evict(sessionId: string): boolean {\n    return this.sessions.delete(sessionId);\n  }\n\n  count(): number {\n    return this.sessions.size;\n  }\n\n  clear(): void {\n    this.sessions.clear();\n  }\n\n  /** Summaries of the user's live sessions, newest activity first, by the same activity `latestFor` reads. */\n  listFor(userId: string | number, options?: SessionListOptions): SessionSummary[] {\n    const limit = cappedLimit(options?.limit);\n    if (limit === 0) return [];\n\n    const ordered = this.ownedBy(userId).map(summaryOf).sort(newestFirst);\n    const before = options?.before;\n    if (before === undefined) return ordered.slice(0, limit);\n\n    const at = cursorAt(before);\n    return ordered.filter(summary => sitsAfter(summary, at, before.sessionId)).slice(0, limit);\n  }\n\n  /** The user's most recently active session that has not aged out: its newest entry decides, or its creation if it has none. */\n  latestFor(userId: string | number): ChatSession | undefined {\n    // Ascending and stable, so of two sessions last active at the same moment, the one created later wins.\n    return this.ownedBy(userId).sort(byActivity).at(-1)?.session;\n  }\n\n  // Their own array, so a caller's sort never reorders the map's insertion order, which the sweep depends on.\n  private ownedBy(userId: string | number): HeldSession[] {\n    return [...this.sessions.values()].filter(held => held.session.userId === userId && !this.hasExpired(held));\n  }\n\n  // Insertion order approximates age order, so the first live entry ends the sweep and the rest cost nothing.\n  private sweepExpired(): void {\n    for (const [sessionId, held] of this.sessions) {\n      if (!this.hasExpired(held)) return;\n      this.sessions.delete(sessionId);\n    }\n  }\n\n  // A session stamped with an unusable clock reading never ages out, rather than expiring immediately.\n  private hasExpired(held: HeldSession): boolean {\n    return Number.isFinite(held.createdAt) && Date.now() - held.createdAt > this.maxAgeMs;\n  }\n}\n\n// Ascending and stable, so latestFor's `.at(-1)` takes the session created later when two are last active {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-5-5","id":"msg_011Cgej4rsbQxkzHz8exs7Bb","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The connection's own session is created in the store on connect, so the list carries that row too. The tests will filter it out by the id the `connected` frame announces. I'm writing two acceptance tests into one new file: the list over a real socket, and the user id coming from the connection rather than the frame."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4888,"cache_read_input_tokens":60407,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":4888},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global","speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b668b808-5dd3-4964-920a-c5d201fff358","uuid":"1e27971b-2221-4f82-afb7-806d4a4b9adc","timestamp":"2026-11-02T20:23:43.189Z","request_id":"req_011Cgej4rewYS1v5H9tJm8MG"}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-5-5","id":"msg_011Cgej4rsbQxkzHz8exs7Bb","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Cw6DB78HwiJ1qqLt7HGsHj","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/14936064dcd7/workspace/target/src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts","content":"import type {WebSocket} from 'ws';\nimport {connectClient, startEngineWith, stopEngines} from './wsTestSupport.js';\nimport {InMemorySessionStore} from '../infrastructure/stores/inMemorySessionStore.js';\nimport type {SessionEntry} from '../types/session.js';\n\n// Acceptance tests for re-cinq/HALEngine#144: a connected client asks the socket for its user's conversations.\n\ninterface Frame {\n  type: string;\n  [key: string]: unknown;\n}\n\nconst HISTORY_ON = {history: {enabled: true}};\n\nconst at = (second: number): string => new Date(Date.UTC(2026, 0, 1, 0, 0, second)).toISOString();\nconst turn = (second: number): SessionEntry => ({role: 'user', content: 'hello', timestamp: at(second)});\nconst isIsoString = (value: unknown): boolean =>\n  typeof value === 'string' && Number.isFinite(Date.parse(value)) && new Date(value).toISOString() === value;\n\n// Every frame the socket receives, from the connect on, until the first answer to a list request.\nfunction framesUntilAnswer(client: WebSocket): Promise<Frame[]> {\n  const seen: Frame[] = [];\n  return new Promise<Frame[]>(resolve => {\n    client.on('message', raw => {\n      const frame = JSON.parse(String(raw)) as Frame;\n      seen.push(frame);\n      if (frame.type === 'conversation_list' || frame.type === 'error') resolve(seen);\n    });\n  });\n}\n\n// The connection's own conversation is listed like any other, so it is set aside by the id its `connected` frame names.\nfunction otherRowsOf(frames: Frame[]): Array<Record<string, unknown>> {\n  const own = frames.find(frame => frame.type === 'connected')?.sessionId;\n  const list = frames.find(frame => frame.type === 'conversation_list');\n  const rows = (list?.conversations ?? []) as Array<Record<string, unknown>>;\n  return rows.filter(row => row.sessionId !== own);\n}\n\ndescribe('a client listing its conversations over the WebSocket', () => {\n  afterEach(async () => {\n    await stopEngines();\n  });\n\n  it('is answered with its user conversations, newest first, with ISO times and no stream_end', async () => {\n    const store = new InMemorySessionStore();\n    store.create('older', 'u1').entries.push(turn(1));\n    store.create('middle', 'u1').entries.push(turn(2));\n    store.create('newest', 'u1').entries.push(turn(3), turn(4));\n    const {url} = await startEngineWith({sessionStore: store, ...HISTORY_ON});\n\n    const client = connectClient(url);\n    const answer = framesUntilAnswer(client);\n    client.once('open', () => client.send(JSON.stringify({type: 'list_conversations'})));\n    const frames = await answer;\n\n    const rows = otherRowsOf(frames);\n    expect({\n      answers: frames.filter(frame => frame.type === 'conversation_list').length,\n      streamEnds: frames.filter(frame => frame.type === 'stream_end').length,\n      rows: rows.map(row => ({sessionId: row.sessionId, entryCount: row.entryCount, updatedAt: row.updatedAt})),\n      timesAreIso: rows.every(row => isIsoString(row.createdAt) && isIsoString(row.updatedAt)),\n    }).toEqual({\n      answers: 1,\n      streamEnds: 0,\n      rows: [\n        {sessionId: 'newest', entryCount: 2, updatedAt: at(4)},\n        {sessionId: 'middle', entryCount: 1, updatedAt: at(2)},\n        {sessionId: 'older', entryCount: 1, updatedAt: at(1)},\n      ],\n      timesAreIso: true,\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('lists the connecting user, never the user named in the frame', async () => {\n    const store = new InMemorySessionStore();\n    store.create('mine', 'u1').entries.push(turn(1));\n    store.create('theirs', 'u2').entries.push(turn(2));\n    const {url} = await startEngineWith({sessionStore: store, ...HISTORY_ON});\n\n    const client = connectClient(url);\n    const answer = framesUntilAnswer(client);\n    client.once('open', () => client.send(JSON.stringify({type: 'list_conversations', userId: 'u2'})));\n    const frames = await answer;\n\n    expect({\n      errors: frames.filter(frame => frame.type === 'error'),\n      sessionIds: otherRowsOf(frames).map(row => row.sessionId),\n    }).toEqual({errors: [], sessionIds: ['mine']});\n  });\n});\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4888,"cache_read_input_tokens":60407,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":4888},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global","speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b668b808-5dd3-4964-920a-c5d201fff358","uuid":"fdb783a0-61ea-4ad5-82a9-047162fa22bf","timestamp":"2026-11-02T20:23:48.232Z","request_id":"req_011Cgej4rewYS1v5H9tJm8MG"}

store.create('older', 'u1').entries.push(turn(1));\n    store.create('middle', 'u1').entries.push(turn(2));\n    store.create('newest', 'u1').entries.push(turn(3), turn(4));\n    const {url} = await startEngineWith({sessionStore: store, ...HISTORY_ON});\n\n    const client = connectClient(url);\n    const answer = framesUntilAnswer(client);\n    client.once('open', () => client.send(JSON.stringify({type: 'list_conversations'})));\n    const frames = await answer;\n\n    const rows = otherRowsOf(frames);\n    expect({\n      answers: frames.filter(frame => frame.type === 'conversation_list').length,\n      streamEnds: frames.filter(frame => frame.type === 'stream_end').length,\n      rows: rows.map(row => ({sessionId: row.sessionId, entryCount: row.entryCount, updatedAt: row.updatedAt})),\n      timesAreIso: rows.every(row => isIsoString(row.createdAt) && isIsoString(row.updatedAt)),\n    }).toEqual({\n      answers: 1,\n      streamEnds: 0,\n      rows: [\n        {sessionId: 'newest', entryCount: 2, updatedAt: at(4)},\n        {sessionId: 'middle', entryCount: 1, updatedAt: at(2)},\n        {sessionId: 'older', entryCount: 1, updatedAt: at(1)},\n      ],\n      timesAreIso: true,\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('lists the connecting user, never the user named in the frame', async () => {\n    const store = new InMemorySessionStore();\n    store.create('mine', 'u1').entries.push(turn(1));\n    store.create('theirs', 'u2').entries.push(turn(2));\n    const {url} = await startEngineWith({sessionStore: store, ...HISTORY_ON});\n\n    const client = connectClient(url);\n    const answer = framesUntilAnswer(client);\n    client.once('open', () => client.send(JSON.stringify({type: 'list_conversations', userId: 'u2'})));\n    const frames = await answer;\n\n    expect({\n      errors: frames.filter(frame => frame.type === 'error'),\n      sessionIds: otherRowsOf(frames).map(row => row.sessionId),\n    }).toEqual({errors: [], sessionIds: ['mine']});\n  });\n});\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01Cw6DB78HwiJ1qqLt7HGsHj","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}
